- 預期毛利率將超越先前指引，估 3Q26 毛利率達 67.6%，EPS
估 29.97 元。 
- 預期管理層將指引 4Q26 營收季增 10%至 12%，毛利率則維
持持平。受惠於下半年強勁表現，預估全年營收將年增 45%至
46%，有望再次超越原先指引。 
- 資本支出方面，預期台積電維持全年 620 億美元的指引，估
計 2027 年及 2028 年資本支出將大幅提升至 850 億及 1,000 億
美元。 
- 2027 年供應缺口將進一步擴大，因此認為台積電將於 2027
年再次調漲晶圓價格，平均漲幅超過 10%，其中先進製程節點
漲價 10-15%，成熟製程節點漲價 5-10%。隨著 ASP 正向趨
勢，認為 FY27 毛利率有望進一步攀升至 70%。

- 預估 2026 年資本支出維持 540 億美元，2027 年則預估達
750~800 億美元。

5%至 10%，成熟製程則上漲 0%至 3%，帶動台積電 2027 年毛
利率有機會接近 70%。

- 預估 3Q26 毛利率 67.5%，EPS 29.07 元。 
- 預估 4Q26 營收季增 11.0%，AI 及 HPC 需求持續強勁，但 N2

32.38 元。 
- 2027 年毛利率預估小幅提升至 67.5%，受惠於價格提升、有
利產品組合、高稼動率及持續生產效率改善。 
- 2026 年資本支出預估維持 640 億美元，但 2027 年及 2028 年
分別上調至 850 億美元及 980 億美元，主要反映設備供應商成
本上升，以及可能新增德州擴廠的初期支出。預估 2027 年底
及 2028 年底 N3/N2 產能分別達到每月 20 萬片/14 萬片及 22
萬片/20 萬片。

- 預估 3Q26 毛利率 67.4%，EPS 33.16 元。 
- 預估 4Q26 營收季增 16%，毛利率 66.7%，EPS 估 28.24 元。 
- 台積電在 AI 運算需求強勁、Agentic AI 潛在上行空間及 CPO
帶動新一輪網路設備週期的支持下，預期 2027 年營收可望年
增超過 40%。隨著 N2 全年貢獻增加，以及 AI 運算工作負載自
2026 年下半年起逐步轉向 N3，並於 2028 年進一步轉向 N2 及
A16。 
- 儘管近期市場傳出台積電可能與德州 Terafab 合作，但台積
電目前已規劃亞利桑那州 6 期先進製程晶圓廠及 2 期先進封裝
產能，管理層過去也曾表示，長期目標是讓亞利桑那州具備約
30%的先進製程產能，以滿足美國需求。第二及第三期預計於
2027 年底至 2028 年開始量產 N3 及 N2，第四至第六期則預計
逐步建置至 2030 年，因此未來幾年暫不將德州新增產能及投
資納入預估。

- 預估 3Q26 毛利率 66.8%，主要受惠於 N3/N5 產能利用率維
持高檔及晶圓漲價，但部分受到 N2 初期量產、海外晶圓廠稀
釋效應及下半年新台幣升值影響。EPS 估 27.92 元。 
- 估 4Q26 營收季增 11%，毛利率 66.8%，EPS 31.37 元。 
- 估 2026 年美元營收將年增 41%，新台幣營收年增 44%，全
年毛利率約 66.9%。 
- 預估 N3 產能將於 2026 至 2028 年底分別達到每月 17.8 萬、
22.3 萬及 25 萬片。N2 方面，台積電為避免重演 N3 供不應求
的情況，正加速擴產，預估 2027 及 2028 年底產能將分別達每
月 11.8 萬及 21 萬片。此外，A14 進度優於原先預期，預估最
快可於 2028 年下半年量產，早於市場普遍預期的 2029 年。 
- 預估 2026 至 2028 年資本支出將分別達 640 億、860 億及
1,000 億美元，其中 2028 年仍有上修空間。 
- 預期 2027 年漲價將涵蓋多數製程，先進製程漲幅約 10%，
N7 及以上成熟製程則調漲低至中個位數百分比。預估 2027 年
整體平均售價將年增 25%，高於 2026 年預估的 17%。

- 預估 3Q26 毛利率 67.1%，EPS 28.47 元。 
- 預估 4Q26 營收季增 10%，受惠於旗艦手機季節性備貨及
HPC 需求加速放量，但考量 N2 初期量產及海外擴廠的成本稀
釋效應，毛利率預估持平於 67.0%，EPS 估 31.26 元。 
- 雲端 AI 長期趨勢持續推動台積電營收成長，預估 2026 年營
收將年增逾 40%，2027 年也可望維持約 40%的成長。近期產
業調查顯示，雲端 AI 需求尚未出現降溫跡象，且 2027 至
2028 年 CPU、GPU 及 ASIC 需求展望進一步轉強。 
- 資本支出方面，維持 2026 年預估 630 億美元，並將 2027 及
2028 年預估由原先的 800 億及 950 億美元，上調至 900 億及
1,050 億美元。主要考量台積電加速擴充 N3 及 N2 產能，並提
前 A14 設備交付時程。將 2028 年底 N3 及 N2 月產能預估分別
上調至 22 萬及 21 萬片。

- 台積電可能與 Terafab 合作的敘事出現變化，此前報道台積

Terafab 項目。

⚫ 上季法說重點(2026/7/16) 
2Q26 法說會重點摘要 
Revenue 
- 3Q26 指引：US$44.6-45.8bn(中值 QoQ+12%、YoY+37%)；匯率假設為 USD/TWD 32 
- 將 2026 年全年美元營收成長預估上調至略高於 40% 
GM 
- 3Q26 指引：65-67%，QoQ-1.7ppts 
- 2Q26 毛利率 67.7%，QoQ+1.5ppts 並略優於公司財測，成本改善措施與整體稼動率略高於預期，
但部分被海外廠初期營運稀釋抵銷 
- 2nm 將於 2H26 快速 Ramp-Up，稀釋毛利率 3~4% 
- 海外廠未來幾年前期每年約稀釋公司毛利率 2~3%，後期擴大到 3~4% 
OP Margin - 3Q26 指引：56-58% 
2Q26 product mix 
- N2 3%；N3 30%；N5 33%；N7 11%；先進製程技術(N7 及以下)占晶圓營收 77% 
- HPC 66%(QoQ+20%)；Smartphone 22%(QoQ-4%)；IoT 5%(QoQ+4%)；Automotive 4%(QoQ+15%)；
DCE 1%(QoQ+5%)

### Page 4

Fubon Catherine

CAPEX&折舊 
- 2026 年 CAPEX 上調至 US$60-64bn(原為 US$52-56bn，接近上緣)；70~80% 於先進製程，10% 特殊
製程、10~20% 先進封裝、測試光罩 
- 資本支出通常對應未來數年更高的成長機會，目前看未來三年 CAPEX 會比過去三年再更為強勁 
- 已提前與設備供應商規畫產能，目前不預期設備供應會成為擴產計畫的重大瓶頸  
- 宣布將在亞利桑那追加 US$100bn 投資(總額提高至 US$265bn)，新增數座 N2 及更先進製程邏輯晶
圓廠、先進封裝廠 
AI 
- 先前提出的 2024-29 年 AI 相關營收「中高 50% CAGR」展望，管理層表示需求已變得「更強、更
強、再更強」，因為展望持續在調高所以不公布數字，實際趨勢高於先前說法  
先進封裝 
- 目前主流方案仍為 CoWoS，同時持續開發其他替代技術以降低封裝成本 
- 對 Intel EMIB-T 等先進封裝競爭，管理層認為後段和前段是完全不同等級，競爭者取得封裝訂單不
代表可切入前段邏輯晶圓

### Page 1

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 1

2026.09.11  
HD 현대중공업 (329180)

드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가  Flash Note 
[조선/기계/방산] 변용진 2122-9181 yongjin.byun@imfnsec.com

Check Point  Buy (Maintain) 
HD현대중공업 중형엔진 및 SMR 공장 증설 결정  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건

목표주가(12M) 860,000 원(유지) 
종가(2026.09.10) 453,500 원 
상승여력 89.6 %

HD현대중공업, 중형엔진 및 SMR 시설투자 공시  
기다리던 엔진 증설의 세부 사항이 드디어 공개되었다. HD 현대중공업은 10일 공시를 통해 총 1조 722억원(중형엔진 
8,336억원/SMR 2,386억원) 규모의 시설투자 계획을 밝혔다. 먼저 중형엔진 관련, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 
확대에 대응 목적으로 생산능력 확충을 위한 토지 취득 및 공장 신설 및 생산설비 구축에 28년 5월까지 투자가 진행될 예정이다. 
중형엔진의 생산 능력은 현재 연간 3GW급에서 증설이 완료되면 7.2GW급으로 확장되며, 첫 엔진 출하는 28년 하반기로 예상된다. 
증설되는 신공장의 가동률은 28년 30%로 출발하여 30년 100%로 올라설 전망이다. 이에 따라 연간 1GW당 약 1조원의 매출과 
20%이상의 영업이익(현 엔진사업부 이상) 수준으로 추정되며, 계획대로 증설이 완료되고 일감을 확보한다면 총 4조원 이상의 매출 
증대 효과가 기대된다. SMR 투자 관련해서는 SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주물량 증가에 대응하기 위해 SMR 전용 공장, 설비 
구축 및 기존 공장 최적화에 29년 4월까지 2,386억원이 투입된다. 이는 연간으로 Terrapower의 Natrium SMR 주기기 2기(각 
345MW)를 제작할 수 있는 규모다. HD현대중공업은 이번 증설이 데이터센터용 엔진만을 위한 것이 아님을 강조했다. 데이터센터 뿐 
아니라 국가전력망, 원전용 비상발전기 등 전세계적으로 증가하는 육상용 발전엔진 수요에 대응하기 위함이며, 최근 미국의 육상 
데이터센터 사업이 님비현상 등으로 일부 좌초되고 있음에도 그보다 더 큰 그림에 베팅한 것이다.  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건  
분명한 기업가치 증분의 근거가 되는 증설공시였음에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업의 주가는 발표 이후 하락으로 마감하였다. 올해 
조선업 주가는 YTD 평균 10%이상 하락하며 약세를 보이고 있는 상태다. (HD현대중공업 -10.0%, 삼성중공업 -11.6%, 한화오션 -
25.5%)  
조선사 공히 2분기의 실적 서프라이즈와 함께 올해 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하는 등 우수한 펀더멘털을 유지하고 있으나 이는 
주가 상승재료로 반영되고 있지 않다. 7~8월 반도체발 주식시장 혼란에 따른 전체적 수급 약세도 무시할 수 없는 요인이다. 여기에 
지난해 주가 상승의 주요 동력으로 작용하였던 미국 함정 사업 관련 협력 진전이 느린 점 등이 주가 약세의 주요 원인으로 
분석된다. 백악관 및 美해군에서는 지속적으로 함정 해외 건조의 필요성을 역설하고 있으나 美입법부에서는 지역구 의원의 반대 
등으로 관련 법안의 입법 동력이 약해져 있는 상황이다. 美국회의 이번 회기(119대 회기, ~2027년 1월 3일까지)가 이미 막바지에 
다다르고 있으며 11월 중간선거 일정까지 감안하면 실질적으로 이번 회기중에 관련 법안이 통과 및 발효될 가능성은 미미하기 
때문에, 2026년 남은 하반기에 함정사업 관련 조선업 주가 동력을 기대하기는 힘들어졌다. 다가오고 있는 3분기 실적도 원화 강세 
및 여름휴가/사고 등에 따른 조업일수 하락, 후판 유통가 상승 등 감익 요인이 산적해 있어 2분기에 비해 쉽지 않은 상황이다.  
그러나 여전히 본업인 조선업의 체력은 훌륭하다. 전세계 선박 발주량은 8월 누적기준 5,972만 CGT로 전년동기 대비 +60.6% 많은 
발주가 나오고 있으며 이는 2021년 이후 가장 강한 발주세다. 한국 조선사의 주력 선종인 LNG, 탱커, 컨테이너선의 발주 비중이 
전체 발주량의 67%를 차지하고 있으며 이에 힘입어 조선사들은 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하였다. (상선기준, HD현대중공업 달성 
130.6억 달러/목표 114.7억, 삼성중공업 달성 61억/목표 57억, 한화오션 달성 60억/목표없음) 3분기 실적은 다소 쉬어갈 수 
있으나 여전히 수주잔고에 기반한 추정상 매출/영업이익은 2028년까지 꾸준한 상승이 예상된다. 작년보다 오히려 하락한 주가가 
현저한 저평가일 수밖에 없는 이유다. 조선업 비중확대 및 HD현대중공업 Top Pick 의견을 유지한다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

Document 150: A reprieve, not a recovery, for EU chemical producers

Source PDF: all report pdf/A reprieve, not a recovery, for EU chemical producers.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 13
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Iran war has hit EU chemicals less severely than feared,
with production, capacity utilisation and margins improving in
recent months. But renewed import competition will cloud the
outlook once Gulf oil and gas supplies to Asia recover. To reverse
the industry's long-term decline, producers and policymakers
must accelerate the green transition
Edse Dantuma
Polyethylene, the world's most common plastic, is used to make everyday
products like packaging ﬁlm, shopping bags, and bottles

Boost from softer Asian competition
The Iran war has hit European chemical producers less severely
than feared. After the ﬁrst tariﬀ shock in early 2025, demand
initially rose as customers brought forward purchases amid supply
chain uncertainty. The main boost, however, came from weaker
imports. Damage to Middle Eastern plants and Strait of Hormuz
blockades sharply reduced production and exports. About a quarter
of regional polyethylene capacity was damaged, with repairs likely

to take months.

### Page 2

Elsewhere in Asia, producers faced prolonged feedstock and energy
shortages. Asia depends far more on Gulf oil, gas and raw materials
than the EU does. Before the conﬂict, Asian countries sourced 40-
90% of their oil from the region, compared with around 20% for the
EU.
Earnings improve strongly, but production
returns to previous level
Reduced competition and tighter supply conditions drove three
consecutive months of growth in EU chemical production from

February to April, while sharp price rises provided a signiﬁcant
boost to second-quarter earnings. But the respite proved short-lived
as output contracted again as inventory restocking faded and
transport links through the Gulf improved.
By June, EU chemical production was 0.5% lower than a year
earlier and remained around 24% below early-2022 levels.
Meanwhile, exceptionally low Rhine water levels continue to disrupt
raw-material shipments and production schedules, particularly in

Germany and the Netherlands.

Spain is Europe’s exception
Germany and the Netherlands have recorded the largest production
declines among the EU’s six biggest chemical producers. Together
with Belgium, they host Europe’s largest petrochemical and basic
chemicals cluster: the Antwerp-Rotterdam-Rhine-Ruhr Area. Highly
energy-intensive processes therefore weigh heavily on their
chemical sectors.
Spain stands out: chemical output has risen relatively strongly this

year and was the only top-six country to record growth in both
2024 and 2025. Its growth over the past decade, despite
ﬂuctuations, is also notable. Spain’s competitive position beneﬁts
from relatively low energy costs, a smaller share of basic chemicals,
a larger consumer chemicals segment, favourable domestic
demand and its location. A pipeline supplies Algerian gas, while EU
recovery funds have helped accelerate the expansion of renewable
power capacity.

Spain's chemical industry has held up
relatively well in recent years

### Page 4

*seasonally adjusted, 2-month moving average
Source: Eurostat
Temporary relief from competition and pressure on basic
chemicals
While the EU chemical industry has broadly been under pressure for
years, performance varies signiﬁcantly across segments. Four of
the ﬁve hardest-hit categories are in basic chemicals, which
produce base products and intermediates. Since early 2022, output
has fallen by between 25% in dyes and pigments and 47% in other
organic basic chemicals. The latter is particularly energy-intensive

and remains heavily reliant on fossil hydrocarbons for both energy
and feedstock. Compared with the previous downturn, basic
chemical producers beneﬁted from weaker international
competition in the ﬁrst half of this year. Only agricultural chemicals
fell sharply, as prolonged drought in the second quarter reduced
demand for crop protection products.
Consumer chemicals, including oils, extracts, perfumes and
toiletries, are the only segments to have grown both since early

2022 and since the start of this year. These downstream products
are less exposed to volatile energy prices and generate more value
through innovation. Speciality chemicals such as paints, coatings
and cleaning products sit between the two: output remains
structurally weak but has declined less than in basic chemicals.
Basic chemicals have been the hardest hit in
recent years

EU chemical activity follows the ebb and
ﬂow of the Iran war
The EU chemical production rebound was short-lived because of the
June US-Iran ceaseﬁre. Transport through the Strait of Hormuz
resumed and energy prices temporarily fell sharply. Chinese oil
reﬁning and exports of oil-based chemicals to the EU recovered. In
July, these exports were almost 40% higher than a year earlier,

while exports of organic basic chemicals – which account for about
two-thirds of China’s chemical exports to the EU – rose by nearly
50%.

Source: Eurostat
Production expectations rise as Middle East conﬂict ﬂares up
again
Since the conﬂict intensiﬁed again in July, Gulf oil, gas and
feedstock shipments crucial to Asian chemical activity have fallen
sharply. Although the switch from oil to coal oﬀers a partial
alternative, this is expected to increase pressure on Asian chemical
production and exports again, which will provide some relief to
European producers.
In August and September, most EU producers therefore expected to

raise output, with expectations reaching a two-year high in
September. European chemicals will beneﬁt from weaker
competition while transport remains disrupted. However,
alternative Gulf routes will probably gain importance if the conﬂict
persists, eventually reviving Asian import competition. Oil
shipments through Hormuz have recently increased to almost 80%
of pre-war levels.

### Page 7

Source: European Commission
Speciality and consumer chemicals have the
strongest outlook
Order books improved slightly again after a brief dip in September.
Yet producers’ assessment of orders remains much further below its
long-term average (-26% versus -14%) than production
expectations, highlighting persistent structural weakness.
The historically wide gap between upstream and downstream
activities has narrowed this year, as have sentiment diﬀerences

between countries. Sentiment improved in basic-chemicals-heavy
Germany, Belgium and the Netherlands, but weakened in France,
Italy and Spain, where basic chemicals play a smaller role. This
reﬂects basic chemicals’ temporarily improved competitive position
but also continued weak demand.
European growth remains constrained by higher inﬂation and only
a limited recovery in end markets. Higher energy prices squeeze
consumer purchasing power, hitting consumer chemicals hardest.

Speciality chemicals have better prospects as investment rises and
the AI boom lifts demand for products and materials used in:
Sustainability and electriﬁcation, including battery materials,
chemical catalysts, ﬂame retardants, lightweight resins and
composites.
Defence equipment, including reinforced plastic ﬁbres,
advanced coatings and lubricants.

### Page 8

Semiconductors, including photosensitive polymers and ultra-

Higher energy and feedstock costs will bite
more gradually
Chemical prices closely track energy costs, so expensive and
volatile energy will also make them higher and more volatile. Yet
the steepest rise in selling prices appears to be over. Although most
producers expected to raise prices in August and September, the
expectations index was stable just below its long-term average – a

far milder increase than after the Iran war began. This suggests
supply is gradually expanding again and price competition is
increasing.
Greater diﬃculty passing on input-cost increases will put margins
under growing pressure in the second half of the year, reinforced as
higher energy costs feed through into purchase prices. Layered
hedging – through long-term contracts, futures and other
instruments – spreads this impact over time and helped margins

improve sharply in the ﬁrst half. Large chemical companies in
particular use rolling hedging strategies, ﬁxing or mitigating prices
for diﬀerent periods: from several months to several years,
depending on the product, company circumstances and market
outlook.
Selling price expectations stayed benign in
September
Selling price expectations for the months ahead, EU chemical industry

### Page 9

Source: European Commission
Structural problems will resurface
The European chemical industry’s structural problems are far from
over. Demand has been weak for years, while competitiveness
continues to erode. European energy prices are substantially higher
than in the US and China, and government support is much lower
than in China. Once transport ﬂows normalise, global overcapacity
will again drive an inﬂux of cheap imports, especially from China.

Global chemical capacity grew by about 15% between 2018 and
2023 and, based on projects already underway, is expected to rise
by another 10% through 2028, keeping structural overcapacity in
place. Over the past decade, the EU’s share of global chemical sales
halved to 13%, while China’s nearly quintupled to 46%. The EU’s
chemicals trade deﬁcit with China widened from €21bn in 2023 to
€24bn in 2025. An artiﬁcially weak Chinese yuan and US import
tariﬀs further undermine Europe’s competitiveness.

Restructuring wave is not over
Despite somewhat better market conditions this year, restructuring
is likely to continue. Most major players have announced strategic
interventions in recent years. Earlier this year, SABIC sold its
European petrochemical operations, while Evonik is implementing a
multi-year plan of divestments, closures and reorganisations. Ineos
plans to close three UK chemical plants, and Shell wants to sell its
entire chemicals division, including a major European cluster.

European chemical capacity shrank by 30 million tonnes, or 7%,
between 2022 and 2026. Announced plant closures increased
sixfold over this period, while investment fell sharply. Petrochemical

Climate costs pose a growing challenge
Climate costs also put Europe at a competitive disadvantage to
countries without carbon pricing or with much lower pricing. As free

allowances are phased out, EU ETS costs will probably weigh
increasingly on production costs towards 2030, both directly
through companies’ own emissions and indirectly through higher
electricity prices. In July 2026, the European Commission proposed
extending the emissions-reduction pathway, modestly easing the
cost increase, alongside more ﬁnancial support for clean-
technology investment.
CBAM oﬀers only partial protection for EU chemicals
Fertilisers and hydrogen are covered by the CBAM levy, which

protects against imports not subject to carbon costs. In
return, free allowances for CBAM products will be phased out

faster, reaching zero by 2034. This will widen the cost gap
between eﬃcient, electriﬁed installations and older fossil-
based plants. Besides the faster phase-out, CBAM has several
drawbacks for chemical companies:
The levy can be circumvented when a chemical feedstock
is covered by CBAM but a product containing it is not.
This may encourage imports of ﬁnished or intermediate
products from outside the EU. The European
Commission’s proposed expansion mainly targets

downstream steel and aluminium products, not the
wider chemical value chain.
Most chemicals remain outside CBAM. Chemical value
chains involve many processes, intermediates and
feedstocks, making embedded emissions diﬃcult to
calculate. Organic chemicals have therefore been
excluded for now.
CBAM does not oﬀset the EU ETS-related competitive
disadvantage faced by EU companies in export markets.
Promising decarbonisation projects remain operationally

challenging
To regain competitiveness, European plants must decarbonise
faster through carbon capture and storage, process electriﬁcation,
and recycled and bio-based feedstocks. Although projects are
regularly cancelled, promising initiatives remain. INEOS plans to
start Project One in Antwerp this year, introducing a modern
ethylene cracker that initially emits less than half as much CO ₂  as
the EU’s most eﬃcient cracker and becomes climate-neutral within

ten years.
Spain’s Repsol aims to decarbonise its Tarragona complex through
circular chemicals and methanol, large-scale green hydrogen and
oﬀshore CO ₂  storage. In September, fertiliser producer Yara began
shipping liquid CO ₂  from Sluiskil in the Netherlands to Norway for
the permanent underground storage of 800,000 tonnes of CO ₂  a

Such projects can quickly cost hundreds of millions of euros a year.

The €5bn Project One has contributed to the doubling of INEOS’s
debt since 2021, while the group suﬀered a loss of more than
€500m in 2025. Chemicals are therefore widely seen as a hard-to-
abate sector. Governments often co-invest, but poor operating
conditions, inadequate green infrastructure and weak demand for
sustainable products still obstruct large-scale decarbonisation.
Infrastructure and decarbonisation
investment are essential
Through its European Chemicals Industry Action Plan, the European

Commission aims to prevent essential chemical production from
leaving the EU. The European Critical Chemicals Alliance brings
policymakers and producers together to identify, monitor, protect
and support critical European production. Industry supports the EU
ETS goals and system, but argues that suﬃcient free allowances
should remain until eﬀective protection against carbon leakage and
adequate demand for sustainable products are in place.
This requires supportive EU policy. Europe’s chemical

transformation is becoming a race against time: more plants will
inevitably close, leaving only the most eﬃcient and fastest-
decarbonising companies and clusters. Brussels’ growing focus on
incentives and safeguards against unwanted international
dependencies therefore oﬀers the sector welcome support.

Goldilocks 
We resume coverage of the PCB/CCL industry, focusing primarily on Google’s TPU supply 
chain, which we believe will deliver the strongest and most sustainable growth through 2028 
and beyond. We forecast aggregate TPU volumes to more than triple between 2025/26 and 
2028, far outpacing competing platforms. Critically, we project a surge in the content value 
of next-generation TPU ASICs. As Google integrates more advanced logic and HBM chiplets 
into a single compute module, we project TPU content value to increase five- to tenfold over 
the next three to four years.  Accordingly, we estimate that the addressable PCB content 
value alone could quadruple from 2026 to 2029. Equally vital is Google’s longstanding 
leadership in optical connectivity for its Superpods. The upcoming doubling of switch speeds 
and interconnect bandwidth will act as a secondary tailwind, further lifting PCB content 
value. Consequently, we expect the PCB/CCL market to experience continuous pricing power 
and a possibility of LTAs forming, mirro ring the supply dynamics seen in the memory 
industry. Within the key TPU vendor matrix, we resume coverage of Isu Petasys and EMC with 
BUY ratings and target prices of KRW 270k and TWD 10,000, respectively. We also initiate 
coverage of TTMI with a BUY rating and a target price of $236. Risks include end demand, 
execution, and US policy on AI data centre expansion. 
Why Google’s TPUs?  
We forecast TPU’s volume to more than triple to 12m units in 2028 (8.5mn in 2027) from 3.6mn 
in 2026, encompassing both in-house deployment and allocations to third-party AI labs via ASIC 
services. The strong volume is evident by its massive $520bn cloud service backlog orders in 
C2Q26. At the same time, TPU is expected to quadruple in TPU V9 (mid-28) vs V8 (2H26) and 
could double again in V10 (2029) as Google squeezes more chiplets into its ASIC designs. The 
complexity of design for PCB (upgrade to 50L+ or HDI) and CCL (upgrade to M8/M9 material or 
even PTFE) represents at least 50%-100% expansion in ASP in each generation. Furthermore, 
networking is expected to see a similar upgrade trend in PCB/CCL, as scale-up architectures 
and bandwidth requirement rise, especially from 400Gbps to 800G/1.6Tbps.    
LTA likely becomes new norm under strong demand and supply constraint  
Our supply chain survey indicated increasing discussion about long-term agreements (LTA) 
over the PCB and CCL supply chain with CSPs. Terms may be different from vendor to vendor, 
and most of them are still under discussion at this stage. However, those discussions send a 
clear message that end users are securing high-end PCB/CCL capacity by prepayment and LTAs 
for the next few years, because the overall industry may continue to remain undersupplied. 
Also, we reckon prepayment 1) will ease supply chain pressure on capex/cash flow, 2) make the 
supply chain more willing to invest given longer visibility and secured shipment, and 3) improve 
supply chain management with better profitability. We believe this will further boost P/E for the 
supply chain, if LTAs are confirmed, with longer visibility and stable order backlogs.   
We prefer TPU supply chain into 2027/28: buy on EMC, ISU and TTM 
The TPU supply chain will be the biggest winner into 2027/28 with strong unit growth and 
content value upgrades. Shipments are expected to reach 8.5/12m in 2027/28 vs 3.6m in 2026. 
Content value per rack for PCBs is up to USD30k-40k and CCL USD12k-18k for TPU v8 (vs 
USD20k/7k in v7). We expect another 40%–50% in v9 with HDI adoption. We like EMC for its 
leading position and expect TPU/800Gbps switches to become the company’s key earnings 
driver. We set our TP at NT$10,000, with 30x FY27/28 PER. ISU is uniquely positioned to capture 
these secular trends due to its high exposure to Google and networking. We have a BUY and a 
TP of KRW270,000 on 30x FY27/28 PER. Lastly, TTM is the best proxy in the United States to 
capture both AI server and national defence trends with better profitability and irreplaceability 
given its industry position. We initiate with BUY with a TP of USD236 with 30x FY27/28 PER. Title

### Page 3

Advanced Materials - PCB & CCL 
Goldilocks

4 October 2026  3 
GPU/ASIC volume, content value and price hikes drive 
PCB/CCL to another strong cycle in 2027/28 
We are positive on the PCB/CCL sector into 2027/28, with a strong upgrade cycle, and the 
whole industry will remain undersupplied because our checks indicate 1) continued AI device 
unit growth, supported by increasing CSP capex, 2) PCB design complexity will boost content 
value (increasing processing time and lowering yield rate), 3) CCL material upgrade to 
support higher data transmission and power supply, and 4) price hikes in the near-term 
because of the supply shortage and LTAs in the longer-term for a better demand outlook. 
What is not in the price yet?  
The sector has outperformed year-to-date, but we believe the PCB/CCL upgrade cycle will 
continue to surprise the market because 1) stronger AI device unit growth (especially Google 
TPU, of which we project 8.5/12m units in 2027/28), 2) significant content value growth via 
PCB design complexity (higher layer counts, advanced form factor like HDI/mSAP, longer 
processing time) and CCL material upgrades (M8/M9, PTFE), 3) wider AI end devices (GPU, 
ASIC, switch, CPU and LPU, for example) will all require high end PCB s to support  
performance and 4) better cash flow and profitability via prepayment and LTAs.  
The overall industry is under a severe supply shortage at this stage, especially high-end 
products, with the key bottleneck being in upstream material (CCL, glass fiber and copper foil) 
as well as PCB equipment, based on our supply chain checks. The longest lead-time for 
certain PCB equipment (like drilling machines) can be up to 36 months, suggesting strong 
surge in demand from the supply chain. We don’t see any sign of the tightness easing in the 
near term.  
AI server unit growth and spec upgrade in 2027/28   
We estimate GPU & ASIC volume will grow approximately 50% in CY2027 and CY2028, 
reaching a potential 34mn units in CY28, up from 15m units in CY26. The key driver is the 
expected unit surge in both CSPs’ ASICs as well as AI labs, that is, OpenAI and Anthropic. Their 
aggregate volume is forecast to rise from 5.5mn –6mn in CY26 to reach 12mn -13mn units in 
CY26 and further increase to 20mn in CY28, implying a quadrupling in a three-year period. 
Some of the notable drivers including the following: 
▶OpenAI’s direct procurement compute power (excluding CPS and neoclouds) is expected 
to reach 11Gw in CY28, up from 3.3GW in CY27 and a mere 1GB in CY26. This includes AVGO’s-
sponsored ASIC of 1.3GW (Jalapeno) and 5GW in CY27/28 respectively, while the remaining 
goes to AMD (8GW in the three-year period) and, to a lesser extent, NVDA; 
▶Similarly, Anthropic’s own compute capacity is likely to reach 13GW in CY28, up sharply 
from CY27’s 6GW. The majority of this comes from AVGO (TPU V8i and V8.5) while 5GW is 
supplied by AMD of  in two years; 
▶Meta is likely to build 3GW of its own compute on its ASIC (via AVGO) in CY27/28 using its 
MTIA400/450 ASIC. We note Meta’s previous ASIC program in 2025/26 has been scaled back 
and pushed out so this marks the second attempt at ramping up its own silicon solution.   
▶We estimate Google’s own compute TPU volume is expected to increase to 5.5mn units in 
CY28 from 4mn -4.5mn in CY27 and ~3.5mn in both CY25/26. While the unit growth appears to 
be modest relative to the AI labs, we note the compute capacity is likely to surge given the 
TDP for V8 and V9 is materially higher than those of V6/7.  
While NVDA’s GPU volume is likely to moderate and market share is expected to decline (as 
most of its customers are building their own ASIC and diversifying to other suppliers), it will 
remain the dominant supplier to most CSPs, neoclouds and enterprises. Moreover, NVDA, 
along with industry is migrating to a multi-rack or large pod structure to offer aggregated

AI server performance to jump significantly every generation 
As shown in the table below, we see huge upgrades in compute power and TDP every 
generation. PCBs and CCL will upgrade along with AI server specs to reduce data loss and 
improve signal performance. PCB design is getting more complex from 20L -30L to 50L+ in the 
future or the structure will likely be migrated from HLT to HDI. For CCL, we witness a clear 
trend of upgrade material from M7 to M8 now and will see more adoption of the M8.5/M9 
going forward, boosting the ASP (a doubling from M7 to M8; M8 to M9 also doubling).  
In the future, along with strong agentic AI demand, we believe AI server design will further 
advance exponentially in the next two to three years. TPU’s TDP is expected to grow 10x from 
v8 to v10 within two to three years to further drive-up PCB/CCL’s content value significantly. 
AMD will equip PCBs with 50L+ into the MI500 generation and Open AI’s aggressive hardware 
production pipeline will provide upside surprise to the market. We can expect PCB/CCL to 
upgrade accordingly. We will have a more comprehensive analysis in the next section.

Summary: PCB/CCL spec upgrade and key suppliers in AI solutions  
The table below shows the key players’ solution and their PCB/CCL specifications as well as 
major suppliers. For PCB, we see an increasing layer count from 20L–30L now to 50L+ in the 
coming years and moving from HLC to HDI for more complex design. Our supply chain check 
suggests AMD’s MI500 series as well as Nvidia’s LPU PCB will likely adopt 50L+ design. This 
represents a longer processing time with a lower yield rate, and design complexity will 
support further ASP hikes. For CCL, M8 is becoming a standard material for AI 
servers/networking and we are going to see more advanced materials into 2027/28 (M9/PTFE). 
CCL is the most critical component in PCB and every generation upgrade (M7 to M8, or M8 to 
M9) implies the ASP will nearly double.    
Nvidia: It started to adopt M8 material from the GB200 NVL72. Rubin is expected to bring 
another modest upgrade: 1) introducing mid-plane with 44L design and M8 (or M8.5) CCL 
material, 2) most of the rest PCB/CCL remain on M8 with higher layer counts. The company is 
evaluating PTFE material for its switch board in the next generation. Also, we think Nvidia’s 
LPU can generate incremental demand for PCB/CCL with its 52L design with M9/M8 materials. 
PCB suppliers for LPU will be VGT, WUS and TTM mainly, while, in our view, CCL suppliers are 
Shengyi (major) and EMC.  
Google: TPU is expected to see the biggest upside in the PCB/CCL addressable market in 
2027/28 thanks to both unit growth and content value upgrades. The next section will have a 
comprehensive analysis.  
Amazon: Trn 3’s air-cooled solution will remain at a 26L PCB design (with M8 material), while 
the liquid-cooled version will migrate to 40L. for Trn3 is solely EMC and TUC are the only CCL 
suppliers for the Trn 3. We expect to see a more significant upgrade for PCB/CCL in the next 
generation Trn 4 (higher layer counts, upgrade to M9?) 
AMD: AMD is another key growth driver for the sector, with meaningful volume into 2027/28.

Google’s TPU to undergo rapid spec upgrades in next 
several years; its PCB/CCL will benefit accordingly  
The figures below illustrate the rapid production transition and specificiation advancement 
of Google’s TPU roadmap in the next several years. We highlight the following considerations: 
▶ The current V8i variant, which is undergoing HVM (through 1H28), receives a modest 
upgrade vs TPU V7 in terms of design and specification. For the latter, there are two logic dies 
and four HBM stacks encompassing TDP of approximately 1,000w. In TPU V8i, the TPD rises 
by 30% with the addition of one logic tile and two HBM stacks.  
▶ We expect the next generation of TPU V9 to undergo a massive uplift in design complexity, 
TPD and compute capability. We estimate there are 12 logic dies (4x times more than V8i) and 
12 HBM stacks (double that of V8i) to be the first to useTSMC’s N2 process node.  Given the 
rapid rise in chiplet quantity, we estimate the reticle size of the design to be 9.5x reticle size. 
The compute capability is likely to surge accordingly with the TDP of both training and 
inference to reach 4,500w, more than triple compared to TPU V8i.  We expect TPU V9 to begin 
HBM in mid-2028 and run through 2029. 
▶ We think TPU V10 will experience the next wave of significant specification and 
performance uplift as the number of logic chiplets rises further to 30, 2.5x vs TPU V9 and they 
are all to be fabicated on TSMC’s N2P geometry as well as being encompassed with 3D 
packaging for the first time. In addition, the number of HBM stacks will rise by one-third to 16 
stacks. We estimate the reticle size is likely to increase to 15x, 50% larger than TPU V9 and 
critically the TDP to surge to 10,000w if not higher!. TPU V10 is expected to enter HBM in 2029.

Implications for PCB/CCL 
We think the TPU supply chain will enjoy the biggest growth in 2027/28 along with strong unit 
growth and content value upgrades among all the GPU/ASICs. Shipments are expected to 
reach 8.5m/12m in 2027/28 vs 3.6m in 2026. We view increasing compute power, higher HBM 
density, and a doubling of ICI bandwidth as pushing PCB designs toward significantly higher 
routing density, tighter L/S, more advanced CCL, smaller via structures, and multi-stage 
lamination to maintain signal integrity and shorten electrical paths.  
We observed that TPU’s PCB ASP has risen 2.5x from TPU V5 to V8, and 4x for the 400Gbps 
switch to 800Gbps. In addition, we estimate 2.6x growth in TPU unit demand from 2025E-27E 
and 3x expansion in the 400bps/800Gbps AI switch market by 2028 versus 2025, or 30% 
growth in total, making TAM growth exponential in these years. 
The table below illustrates the structure of Google TPU v8. One rack will contain eight 
compute trays, with each tray equipped with four TPUs, four NICs, four optical modules as 
well as two CPUs (Axiom). TOR (Top-Of-Rack)/OCS will connect those racks together to form 
a superpod (via the Virgo network) and the Juniper network to connect with other functions. 
The increasing compute power and connectivity represents significant content value growth 
for PCB/CCL based on those performance upgrades and data transmission.  
To support the performance upgrades, TPU PCB architecture is shifting from standard 
multilayer (Ghostfish) to N+M structures using dual -grade CCL and multi-lamination in 
Zebrafish and Sunfish, and ultimately to HDI PCB in Humufish. These transitions lift PCB ASPs 
by 30% in Ghostfish, 80%–90% in Sunfish/Zebrafish. Based on our estimates, content value 
per rack for PCB is up to USD30k-40k and CCL USD12-18k for TPU v8 (vs USD20k/7k in 
v7). We expect another 40%–50% in Humufish with HDI adoption. Into TPU V10, given 
that TDP will further double to 10kW, we can assume PCB/CCL will be further upgraded 
to meet the higher requirement.

N+M sequential lamination is inherently more challenging than a standard 
multilayer build because it requires multiple lamination cycles, yield rates could 
fall by 15%-30%.

its high density, but it is more challenging 
than N+M because it requires multiple 
cycles of laser drilling, sequential

Source: Aletheia/TAG 
We see an increasing trend for adopting HDI in advanced AI acclerators: 1) it shortens high-
frequency signal paths, reducing signal attenuation and latency, 2) it increases circuit density 
through microvia technology, including blind and buried vias, 3) it saves internal space and 
4) it optimizes heat dissipation efficiency. HDI normally requires fewer layers compared to 
conventinal multi-lam PCB due to its higher density, but HDI PCB is still more challenging 
because it requires multiple cycles of laser drilling, sequential buildup, and ultra-fine feature 
formation. The longer processing time and lower yield rate will further drive up content value 
for PCB.

Increasing networking growth will be another pillar of growth 
ToR (Top‑of‑Rack) switch demand for AI server systems is set to grow the fastest during 2026–
28 as architectures scale-up and bandwidth requirements rise. As scale ‑up bandwidth 
increases, data move outward more quickly, shifting bottlenecks in the scale‑out network. 
Google’s TPU v8 network architecture is a good example as illustrated below.  
FIGURE 12: MLCC DEMAND IN VARIOUS AI SERVERS AND GENERAL SERVERS 
Source: Aletheia/TAG 
Adoption of 800 Gbps switches is set to accelerate in 2026–27, with CCL moving from M7 to 
M8 and PCB architecture shifting from multilayer to N+M. As a result, we estimate the 800 
Gbps switch PCB ASP will rise ~275% to ~USD1,500 from USD350 –400 in 400 Gbps 
systems. The layer count will increase by 35%–40%, with the remaining value uplift driven 
by higher‑grade materials and more complex structures. For 1.6T switches, PCB ASP is

### Page 10

Advanced Materials - PCB & CCL 
Goldilocks

4 October 2026  10 
expected to reach USD3,000–4,000 as the layer count expands to 44–50L and CCL upgrades 
to M9 (potentially mixed with M8 in power layers). 
At the Hot Chips Conference, a premier annual semiconductor industry event, at Stanford 
University at the end of August, Google further disclosed the networking architecture for its 
upcoming TPU8t (Zebrafish – MediaTek) and TPU8i (Sunfish – Broadcom). In addition to 
upgrading its Jupiter network (north-south traffic) architecture, which connects storage, 
general servers, and its global WAN with higher bandwidth, lower latency, and fewer hops, 
Google also detailed its new Virgo network ( east-west traffic). Through the co-design of 
network architecture, optical switching technology, and the software orchestration layer, the 
Virgo network facilitates scaling the maximum TPU cluster size to 1m units (up from 100,000 
units in TPU v7 – Ironwood).  
This upgrade highly relies on networking hardware advancements, particularly in optical 
communications. Our deep-dive research into Google’s new TPU network architecture leads 
to the conclusion that demand will increase for ethernet switches and optical transceivers.

Who will benefit most in the Google TPU supply chain?  
ISU Petasys is best positioned to capture this growth given its high exposure to networking 
PCBs (50%–60% of sales) and its role as a key PCB supplier to Google (50%+ of sales). We also 
consider EMC another great proxy for the TPU supply chain, because  of 1) its leading position 
in high-end CCL, 2) its increasing market share in ASICs (Google, AWS) as well as AMD, and 3) 
its better margin/profitability along with favorable product mix and price hike s. TTM is 
another key supplier to Google TPU for N+M products, and we expect its business to 
accelerate from 2H26. If Google confirms LTAs with its PCB/CCL vendors, we think it will 
further drive-up the supply chain’s P/E with more stable backlogs and longer order visibility.  
Price hikes will continue; LTAs are ongoing  
Another key growth driver to lift PCB/CCL makers’ profitability is price hikes. We have 
witnessed several rounds of price hikes already this year and expect more. Besides, our 
channel check suggests that CSPs are working closely with PCB/CCL makers for LTAs to 
secure advanced capacity and smooth the production process. This section will discuss these 
two issues and implications for the supply chain.  
Price hikes will likely continue  
Our supply chain checks suggest that CCL makers are increasing prices by 50%-100% YTD to 
reflect 1) organic material upgrades under the supply constraint, 2) a shortage inf upstream 
materials supply and price hikes (resin, copper foil and glass cloth), and 3) longer and more 
complex laminate process for more advanced CCL. We see price hikes across the board from 
all production lines from M8 material to M4  because tier 1 makers are allocating more 
capacity for advanced materials (which requires a longer processing time resulting in lower 
production volume). As such, entry-level products will be excluded and will partially shift to 
second-tier vendors.  
Based on current visibility, we expect more waves of price hikes in CCL in 2H26 and 1H27. We 
think each round will be 10%-15%. We assume better profitability for CCL makers in future 
quarters owing to 1) higher UTR under a supply constraint, 2) continued price hikes and 3) 
favorable product mix along with product migration.  
Are LTAs becoming new norm for PCB/CCL?  
Given the tight supply conditions and strong PCB/CCL demand for the foreseeable future, we 
are also hearing that CSPs are aggressively approaching PCB and CCL makers for LTAs to 
secure capacity. Terms will vary from vendor to vendor. However, those discussions send a 
clear message that end users are securing high-end PCB/CCL capacity by prepayment and 
LTAs for the next few years, because the overall industry may continue to remain 
undersupplied. Also, we reckon prepayment 1) will ease supply chain pressure on capex/cash 
flow, 2) make the supply chain more willing to invest under longer order visibility and secured 
shipment volume, and 3) improve supply chain management with better profitability. Below 
are typical LTAs between memory suppliers and their key customers.

New trends/developments in PCB supply chain and 
implications  
Given the fast development of PCB design, there is lots of noise in the supply chain about the 
future PCB demand outlook. This section will discuss the latest developments in the PCB 
industry and their implications for the supply chain, including mSAP adoption in optical, pros 
and cons for PTFE material (hybrid/pure) as well as CPO’s impact on the CCL supply chain.    
mSAP is becoming a standard for optical beyond 1.6T  
mSAP (Modified Semi-Additive Process) is not a new technology. It is an advanced PCB 
manufacturing method that builds fine circuit traces by electroplating copper onto a thin 
layer rather than etching away thick copper. It allows PCB to further shrink its L/S to below 
30um (compared to 30-50um for traditional etching). As such, PCB  can support higher 
density to support advanced electronic devices with a smaller/complex form factor.  
FIGURE 16: MSAP PROCESS IS SUITABLE FOR PCB WITH L/S <30UM  
  Standard HDI process        
L/S > 30um

Filling laser Cu plating Dry film lamination Copper etching Dry film striping 
  mSAP       
L/S <30um

Dry film lamination Filling laser Cu plating Dry film striping Flash etching 
Source: Aletheia/TAG 
To meet the requirement for higher integration and miniaturization, optical modules are 
increasingly adopting mSAP to reduce product size and signal loss. Our supply chain check 
suggests that 1) we are seeing some 800G switch mSAP adoption, but it is more like an 
optional solution to ally with a specific form factor according to a vendor’s requirement, and 
2) mSAP is becoming a mandatory process from 1.6T given the L/S is expected to shrink to 
below 30um.  
FIGURE 17: MSAP IS BECOMING A STANDARD FROM 1.6T  
  400G 800G 1.6T 3.2T 
Layer (HDI) 10L 12-14L 14-18L 20-22L 
L/S 40-50um 30-40um 20-30um 10-15um

Implications for supply chain: The key bottleneck for mSAP is more in the 1) upstream 
materials from Japan vendors, including carrier copper foil from Mitsui Kinzoku and Nittobo’s 
low-CTE glass fabric and 2) selected equipment like laser drilling machines and laser direct 
imaging (LDI) machines. The production lead-time has increased to 10-12 months (vs the 
normal 4-6 months).  
For PCB supply, we believe SCC, ZDT and Unimicron will be the key beneficiaries  of mSAP. 
Compeq is also being qualfied for its mSAP solution for 800G/1.6T switches, based on our 
checks.   
FIGURE 18: MAJOR PLAYERS’ PROGRESS WITH MSAP   
SCC 
SCC is a leader in the mSAP process among Chinese PCB makers. The company 
recently announced a RMB4.37bn private placement to expand AI-related PCB 
capacity (including mSAP). 
ZDT/Avary 
ZDT/Avary is aiming to become the world's largest mSAP supplier by expanding 
capacity 5x in the coming years at all its major production sites (including China, 
Taiwan and Thailand).  
Unimicron 
Unimicron raised its 2026 capex from TWD25bn to TWD34bn in its latest earnings 
call. According to the company, 70% will be used in ABF substrate and 30% for HDI, 
mSAP and new production site in Thailand. 
Compeq 
Compeq has passed qualification for mSAP in high-end switch products and started 
to enter into mass production from 2H26. The company will continue to expand 
mSAP capacity in both its Taiwan and China (Chongqing) sites.  
 Source: Aletheia Capital, TAG 
Potential PTFE developments in the future 
Nvidia is testing hybrid material (PTFE + M9) in the Rubin Ultra generation for switch trays to 
meet up with higher requirements of data transmission and may consider pure PTFE material 
for the backplane in the next generation. CCL makers are testing and sampling several 
solutions at this stage, and we will have better visibility into 1H27.  
FIGURE 19: PCB’S MAJOR RESIN MATERIAL DK/DF COMPARISON: PTFE HAS BEST DK/DF

### Page 15

Advanced Materials - PCB & CCL 
Goldilocks

4 October 2026  15 
M9 materials based on PPO/HC resin are no longer sufficient to meet Nvidia’s performance 
requirements. PTFE has better/stable signal performance (low Dk/Df) and the current hybrid 
solution with no glass cloth can reduce the usage of Q-glass, which is under a supply 
constraint. However, the hybrid solution is difficult to process (laminate/drill), making its 
yield rate very low and higher manufacturing costs. Also, the new material will take longer 
time for qualification.  
Implication to supply chain: We don’t think PTFE will become mainstream material in AI 
servers in the near future considering its low yield rate and high cost. It is more likely to be 
adopted in areas which require extremely low Dk/Df like Nvidia is testing it for switch boards 
and potentially backplane.  
For PTFE CCL supply, we think Shengyi will be the first source given its track record with 
Nvidia. EMC/Taiflex camp will likely be the second source potentially (EMC supplies M9 PP 
and Taiflex supplies PTFE CCL). For PCB supply, we think the major suppliers will be Chinese-
based PCB vendors (SCC, Kinwong, WUS and VGT for example) and the yield rate is still low 
(below 30%).  
CPO may trigger CCL de-spec? 
CPO is an advanced heterogeneous integration of optics and silicon devices in a single form. 
It enables the integration of both PIC and EIC and packages them in a modular form factor 
along with switches or XPU (including ASIC) via substrate or interposer to support high-speed 
transmission and power saving. In traditional systems, electronic signals are converted to 
optical signals with signal loss and higher power consumption. CPO, however, places the 
optical engines closer to the switch ASIC or xPU, reducing the distance that signals need to 
travel and improving the overall performance. 
Most common data transmission involves using a transceiver module to input data into the 
switch ASIC for computing, which must pass through the PCB. For switches, the PCB distance 
between the transceiver module and the switch ASIC is approximately 11 to 12  inches or 
±300mm. This results in signal loss and reduces the switch ASIC’s signal integrity from 40 dBm 
to 20 dBm, implying a 50% signal loss. This is despite industry efforts to develop advanced 
low Df/Dk CCL to reduce signal loss and latency. As such, placing the optical engine next to 
the switch IC will efficiently resolve this problem as illustrated in figure below. 
FIGURE 20: CPO ADOPTION LIKELY TO SHRINK THE USAGE OF ADVANCED PCB/CCL

Source: Aletheia/TAG

### Page 16

Advanced Materials - PCB & CCL 
Goldilocks

4 October 2026  16 
Implication for the supply chain: The CPO’s purpose is to minimize the data transmission 
on the PCB, allowing high-speed data transmission to take place within the IC level. In this 
case, PCB in switch systems may no longer require the low-Df/Dk CCL material compared to 
its use of high-end low Df/Dk CCL (M6-M8) for 400Gbps and 800Gbps switch today. As such, 
some people may be concerned that the adoption of CPO will slow down the further 
migration of CCL.  
More impact on CCL side but manageable  
As discussed above, we believe the adoption of CPO will impact the CCL side more (material 
change) instead of the PCB side. However, we don’t think it will become an immediate threat 
and the overall impact should be manageable, because 1) The design of CPO is not finalized 
and still faces challenges in higher manufacutring costs, lower yield rate, and reliability issues, 
2) Nvidia is more aggressive in CPO solutions while other vendors (like Google) still stick with 
existing solutions, and 3) the overall CCL industry is undersupplied and material upgrades of 
more and more new applications are taking place in to fulfill future demand.  
Hence, our conclusion is 1) CPO penetration takes time and the initial impact will be more in 
the Nvidia supply chain instead of the entire AI supply chain for CCL makers, and 2) the overall 
trend for CCL material upgrades will remain strong as other non-CPO application demand 
remains strong and the overall industry is still undersupplied.

More to Come 
We transfer coverage and reiterate BUY for ISU and maintain a TP at KRW270,000 on 30X 
FY27/28E PER. We remain optimistic for its accelerated revenue and earnings growth into 
2028E, driven by increasing PCB design complexity and accelerating deployment of 
Google’s TPU and expanding demand in high ‑speed networking. We believe the 
exponential growth compounded by both unit growth and content value upgrades will 
continue to lead to the growth of its AI PCB business and offset the concerns about market 
share loss. Not to mention there will be more ASIC projects to come to further boost 
growth and diversify its concentration. We view ISU as the best proxy to capture solid 
growth from both Google TPU and networking upgrades thanks to its high exposure, as 
well as leading high‑end PCB technology and strategic geographic presence in Korea. 
TPU to lead growth, but more to come  
We expect ISU’s revenue/earnings to increase sequentially from 2H26 to the entire 2027 
along with the Google TPU v8 ramp up. Despite concerns about market share loss, we view 
that 1) the absolute shipment/revenue contribution from Google will still be more than 
double, considering the TAM is growing 4-5x with higher unit shipment (3.6m to 8.5/12.0m) 
and higher content value (80%-150% higher) for both of compute and networking, and 2) less 
involvement in Google’s general server business. The company is also working on customer 
diversification; MSFT’s ASIC will become ISU’s next key growth driver from 2028 onward.  
New capacity is on the way to support future growth  
ISU is expanding capacity aggressively to meet the strong demand: 1) an earlier Fab 6 ramp 
in 1Q27 for a 25% capacity increase, 2) A 20-25% capacity increase by 2Q28 after Fab 5 
expansion for HDI to secure annual sales capacity of W2tn, according to the company. Fab 7 
is also under discussion if HDI demand comes in stronger than expected. Its core MLB (multi-
lam board) products will remain in strong demand while new HDI products are under 
development. The company doesn’t believe the increasing adoption of HDI will cannibalize 
demand for MLB. Also, we see continued price hikes (along with upstream material shortage 
and a strong end demand outlook) and better product mix will drive ISU’s profitability to 
further expand in FY27/28 under a higher UTR.  
BUY with unchanged TP of KRW270,000 
ISU is uniquely positioned to capture two major trends due to its high exposure to Google 
and networking. We project its EPS will triple from FY26 to FY28 with larger revenue scale and 
a favorable product mix. We have a BUY and an unchanged TP of KRW270,000 on 30x FY27/28 
P/E. We roll over our valuation period to the average of 2027/28 with longer order visibility 
and more AI PCB projects to come, while we lower our multiple slightly from 35x to 30x to 
align with regional peers.

based on SOTP. We anticipate TTM earnings will double from FY26E to FY28E, driven by 
strong AI demand (N+M) and a solid footprint in aerospace & defense (A&D). TTM’s data 
center and networking segment will enjoy 79% CAGR sales in 2026-28 along with TPU v8 
ramping up. We believe its leading N+M position and franchise in the US will allow TTM to 
secure more AI server opportunities (like Meta) in the future . The company is also 
expanding its capacity aggressively in both Asia and the USA to capture more value in both 
AI and A&D space. 
Leading PCB maker in the US  
TTM is one of the world’s top 10 PCB makers and No.1 in the USA, as well as a total solutions 
provider for aerospace & defense (A&D) systems. We think its strong franchise in the USA and 
global footprint (capacity in North America, China and Southeast Asia) can help the company 
to seize opportunities in 1) content value upgrades in AI PCBs from Google and other ASICs, 
2) increasing spending in the A&D area and its tight relationship with the US government, and 
3) organic growth in medical and automotive spaces and continued M&A strategy.

and raised full-year guidance, suggesting this segment would double in 2026 with strong 
N+M demand. We project 2H26 AI-related growth will accelerate at 150%-200% YoY along 
with TPU ramping up to meet company guidance. As one of the major PCB suppliers to 
Google TPU, we see huge upside in this business: 1) TPU’s PCB ASP has lifted 2.5x from TPU 
V5 to V8 with more complex design and upgrades, and 2) we estimate TPU unit demand will 
grow from 3.6m in 2026 to 8.5/12.0m in 2027/28. Thanks to its strong capability in high-end 
PCBs, we believe TTM will penetrate more AI projects in the future (like Meta’s ASIC).  
EPS to double from FY26-FY28E; initiate with BUY and a USD236 TP  
We forecast TTM earnings will double from FY26E to FY28E with better product mix and 
increasing AI exposure. We expect increasing demand to accelerate capacity additions, 
supported by TTM’s leadership in high‑end PCB technology, its long‑term relationship with 
key customers and unique industry position (the largest US-based PCB maker). We initiate 
with a BUY and a target price of USD236 by using a 27x FY27/28 average PER. We use SOTP to 
derive our target multiple as we think it will better capture its dual growth drivers (AI and 
A&D), as its irreplaceability, in our view, make TTM the best proxy in the US to capture both

To support the performance upgrades, TPU PCB architecture is shifting from standard 
multilayer (Ghostfish) to N+M structures using dual -grade CCL and multi-lamination in 
Zebrafish and Sunfish, and ultimately to HDI PCB in Humufish. These transitions increase PCB 
ASPs by 30% in Ghostfish, 80%–90% in Sunfish/Zebrafish. Based on our estimates, content 
value per rack for PCBs is up to USD30k-40k and CCL USD12k-18k for TPU v8 (vs USD20k/7k 
in v7). We expect another 40%–50% in Humufish with HDI adoption. Into TPU V10, given that 
TDP will further double to 10kW, we can assume that PCB/CCL will be further upgraded to 
meet the higher requirement.

 Multi-layer Multi-layer N+M N+M HDI HDI

N+M sequential lamination is inherently more challenging than a standard 
multilayer build because it requires multiple lamination cycles, yield rates could 
see 15-30% loss 
HDI PCB requires less layers owing to its 
high density, but it is more challenging 
than N+M because it requires multiple 
cycles of laser drilling, sequential 
buildup and ultra fine feature formation. 
That would see further yield rate loss. 
ASP Index 1 1.3 2.2 2.1 2.5 ~5-6 
Source: Aletheia Capital, TAG 
We see an increasing trend for adopting HDI in advanced AI acclerators: 1) it shortens high-
frequency signal paths, reducing signal attenuation and latency, 2) it increases circuit density 
through microvia technology, including blind and buried vias, 3) it saves internal space and 
4) it optimizes heat dissipation efficiency. HDI normally requires fewer layers compared to 
conventional multi-lam PCB due to its higher density, but HDI PCB is still more challenging 
because it requires multiple cycles of laser drilling, sequential buildup, and ultra-fine feature 
formation. The longer processing and lower yield rate will further drive up content value for 
PCB.  
We believe its leading N+M position and franchise in the US allows TTM secure more AI server 
opportunities in the future. Apart from Google, we reckon TTM is also one of the key suppliers 
for Nvidia switch board PCBs and upcoming LPU, which can generate incremental demand 
for PCB/CCL with its 52L design with M9/M8 materials. Also, our supply chain suggests TTM is 
qualified to be one of the major PCB suppliers for Meta’s ASICs (MTIA series), which will have 
more meaningful volume in the coming years. 
Driver 2: A&D and medical segment growth from both organic and external  
Aerospace & Defense represented approximately 46% of 2025 revenue and has become one 
of TTM's most important structural growth platforms. According to the company, the current 
program backlog of A&D has reached an historical high level of USD1.7bn. TTM's A&D strategy 
is to move vertically through the electronics supply chain  from PCB/components to 
module/system solution providers as shown below. This vertical integration is strategically 
important because TTM can potentially increase its dollar content as customers move from 
individual components toward increasingly integrated electronic systems.  
FIGURE 7: TTM’S VERTICAL INTEGRATION INTO SYSTEM LEVEL

Source: Aletheia Capital, TAG 
TTM is investing heavily to expand North American capacity for A&D, including ultra HDI, 
munitions acceleration, defense growth, PCB reshoring and system design & integration.  
Given the sensitivity of the business, it is critical to build capacity in the US for core 
components. Management expects these investments to enable more than USD500mn of 
incremental projected throughput by 2028.

Source: Company and Aletheia Capital, TAG 
Apart from organic growth, the company is also using M&A to aggressive expand its A&D 
business. In August 2026, TTM announced it would acquire Epiq, a solution provider of RF 
products, space compute, and electronic warfare applications, for approximately USD$1.1bn 
(see the press release for more details). The acquisition will elevate TTM’s vertical integration 
capabilities for its integrated electronics products and expands its addressable opportunities 
in high-priority defense mission areas. According to the company, the acquisition is expected 
to be immediately accretive to EBITDA margins and accretive to non-GAAP diluted EPS during 
2028 for the A&D business.  
Earlier this year, TTM also announced two acquisitions: Swiss Technology Group (STG) & ILFA. 
Both are European PCB companies (see the press release for more details). We view it is a 
milestone for TTM in establishing its Europe footprint. STG is a Swiss-based PCB company 
focused on medical (surgical robotics, hearing aids, medical imaging and implantable 
solutions). Its focus on miniaturized and small form-factor technology is also highly relevant 
for TTM’s A&D business. ILFA has a 45-year legacy as a German provider of complex PCB 
solutions spanning A&D, industrial, and medical technology markets.

BUY: a unique proxy standing for USA PCB  
We view TTM as a unique proxy among all PCB peers given its presence in the US with leading 
advanced capacity and tight relationships with key customers. AI PCB will lead the growth at 
a 75% CAGR in 2026-28 and boost the group sales CAGR at 39%. We forecast TTM earnings will 
double from FY26E to FY28E with better product mix and increasing AI exposure. We estimate 
ROE will expand to 26%/32% in 2026/27. 
We expect increasing demand to accelerate capacity additions, supported by TTM’s 
leadership in high‑end PCB technology, its long‑term relationship with key customers and 
unique industry position (the largest US-based PCB maker). In the meantime, the continued 
investment in high-end PCB (ultra HDI) and system integration will further secure TTM ’s 
leading position in the A&D space as one of the few key suppliers. We initiate with a BUY and 
a target price of USD236 by using a 27x FY27/28 average PER.

Leader in CCL 
We transfer coverage and reiterate BUY and set a NT$10,000 target price for EMC, based 
on 30x FY27/28E P/E, supported by continued material upgrades and price hikes for high-
end CCL. EMC is the best proxy to seize strong upside from ASIC AI into production ramping 
up (exposure to both Google TPU and AWS Trn3) into 2H26/2027. We estimate TPU unit 
demand will grow from 3.6m in 2026 to 8.5/12.0m in 2027/2028. EMC’s capacity will double 
by the end of 2028 (vs ‘25) to support a strong demand pipeline and new AI CCL 
opportunities (more ASIC/GPU projects, HDI adoption and PTFE, for example) We think 
EMC’s leading position will be more secured when migrating to more advanced CCL 
materials with fewer qualified suppliers.  
Unstoppable upgrade trend in CCL  
We believe the CCL upgrade cycle will continue to surprise the market because 1) stronger AI 
device unit growth (especially Google TPU we project 8.5/12m units in 2027/28), 2) significant 
content value growth via CCL material upgrades (M8/M9, PTFE), 3) wider AI end devices (GPU, 
ASIC, switches, CPU and LPU, for example) will all require high end PCB/CCL to support 
performance and 4) better cash flow and profitability via prepayment and LTAs. The overall 
industry is under a severe supply shortage at this stage, especially high-end products, with 
the key bottleneck in upstream material (CCL, glass fibre and copper foil) to trigger further 
rounds of price hikes. We believe EMC’s leading position will become more solid when 
migrating to more advanced CCL materials (M8/M9) with 1) fewer and less qualified 
suppliers, and 2) additional capacity ready. 
Capacity to double by end of 2028 (vs 2025) to support strong growth  
EMC’s capacity will double by the end of 2028 (vs 2025) to support a strong demand pipeline 
and new AI CCL opportunities, including 1) further CCL upgrade s in TPU v9/Trn 4, 2) 
increasing adoption of HDI in AI accelerators to benefit EMC, 3) more ASIC/GPU projects like 
AMD/Meta, for example. M8 is becoming a standard material for AI servers/networking and 
we will see more advanced materials into 2027/28 (M9/PTFE). CCL is the most critical 
component in PCB and every generational upgrade implies the ASP will nearly double. 
Buy with new TP at NT$10,000 
We see a clear trend of CCL upgrades continuing into 2028 based on current design roadmaps 
and product pipelines. Its industry-leading position in high-end CCL and new capacity means 
EMC is the best proxy for upside from ASIC AI into production ramping up. We reiterate BUY 
and set a new TP at NT$10,000 with 30x FY27/28 PE. We roll over our valuation period to the 
average of 2027/28 with longer order visibility and more AI CCL upside for upgrades.

Document 176: Anthropic信用评级曝光：BB+，最高级别的垃圾级信用

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic信用评级曝光：BB+，最高级别的垃圾级信用.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic ⾸获信⽤评级 BB+ ，最⾼级别的投机级（垃圾级）评分，仅⽐投资级低⼀档。评
级机构 KBRA 在信披极为有限的情况下，以 " ⻛险评分 " 替代完整财务模型完成评估。但
KBRA 相较于穆迪、标普和惠誉体量更⼩，⽽后三者将最终决定 Anthropic 上市后的正式信
⽤评级。
摘要：
AI 独⻆兽 Anthropic 的信⽤评级⾸次浮出⽔⾯。
据英国《⾦融时报》获得的⽂件显⽰，信⽤评级机构 Kroll Bond Rating Agency （ KBRA ）于今
年 6 ⽉授予 Anthropic BB+ 发⾏⼈评级 —— 即最⾼级别的投机级（垃圾级）评分，仅⽐投资级低
⼀档。该评级在 Anthropic 完成⼀笔由 Apollo 主导、规模达 350 亿美元的芯⽚融资交易之前获得，
此前从未公开披露。
这⼀评级对 Anthropic 的融资前景具有直接影响。华尔街各⼤银⾏正在筹备⼀笔 600 亿美元的新债
务⽅案，以帮助 Anthropic 收购更多由⾕歌和博通联合开发的定制 AI 芯⽚，其中包括 180 亿美元的

次级债务。能否跨越投资级⻔槛，将决定 Anthropic 能否以更低成本接触寿险公司、养⽼基⾦等
更⼴泛的机构投资者群体。
评级细节：有限信披下的 " ⻛险评分 "
KBRA 授予的 BB+ 评级附有稳定展望，针对的是 350 亿美元融资包中约 44 亿美元的次级债务 ——
这部分债务将投资者直接暴露于 Anthropic 的信⽤⻛险之下；其余部分则由芯⽚制造商博通提供
担保。该笔次级债以约 8.5% 的收益率发⾏，⾼级债的利率则为 5.75% 。
这⼀评级是在信息披露相当有限的情况下作出的。
据⽂件显⽰， KBRA 获得的财务信息包括营收、调整后营业收⼊以及 2026 年第⼀季度年化运营
收⼊，以及 Anthropic 提供的第⼆季度业绩内部预测，但⾃由现⾦流及 EBITDA 等关键财务指标均
未纳⼊披露范围。评级分析师不得不依赖假设⾃⾏估算，并最终采⽤ " ⻛险评分 " ⽅式替代传统的
完整财务模型来评估其信⽤状况。
财务表现：增速放缓，但⾼于预期
Anthropic 在周三举⾏的 IPO 前投资者推介会上披露，第三季度收⼊环⽐增⻓近 50% ⾄ 168 亿美

元，并连续两个季度实现调整后盈利。
不过，这⼀增速较年初有所放缓，最新财务数据也低于部分最乐观的市场预期。
在评级决定作出时， Anthropic 曾预测第⼆季度收⼊为 109 亿美元，据知情⼈⼠透露，实际数字⾼
于预期，达到 115 亿美元。截⾄ 6 ⽉底，这家成⽴五年的公司⾃⾝资产负债表上债务为零，此前
⼀直依靠信⽤状况更佳的业务合作伙伴及特殊⽬的载体进⾏融资。
据知情⼈⼠透露， Anthropic 还可能发⾏最多 420 亿美元的可转换票据，⽤于⽀付向博通租赁半导
体的费⽤，但 KBRA 未获提供该可转换票据的详细条款。
评级竞赛：三⼤机构才是最终裁判

The Japan Research Institute, Limited

Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

### Page 3

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.

a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social

components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to

companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.
